March 29, 2024 01:30 AM GMT

# Investor Presentation - India Technology and Telecoms | Asia Pacific Key Themes and Our Order of Preference

Internet - We believe that earnings momentum, which has held up well over the past few quarters, should support multiples in the India Internet segment.

IT Services - Post ACN's results, we have moderated our growth estimates and lowered sector EPS/PTs. A better macro set up (possibility of bounce-back in 2H), improving commentary from hyperscalers and investor positioning keep us neutral to positive, thereby holding up our multiples.

Telecom - Prefer INFY/TCS/HCLT. We also see limited upside potential in the India Telecoms industry.

## What are global IT companies’ guidance trends suggesting?

Bottoming out of revenue growth (yoy cc growth %): Growth is bottoming out but pace of recovery has come down vs previous estimates

![](images/75c0e7695bd4196922f93210139e3c973be8a2f1c04276b232e5cbc73dfcd80c.jpg)

## Revenue growth recovery to be a gradual one unlike in previous cycles such as post-GFC and post Covid-19

IT Services: How is the current cycle different from the past? The slowdown was more gradual in 2023, and we expect the recovery to be slower than previous phases post-GFC and Covid-19; however, unlike 2016, we see the underlying technology cycle supporting recovery

![](images/c8303c8a2859c9493b498c4e6f7429c1597783982f3e192b23a536174e0765ac.jpg)
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates. Note: Above data represents constant currency revenue growth YoY for Infosys and TCS. For 4Q08 to 1Q11, growth rates for TCS are in USD terms and not in CC terms.

## Bharti Airtel: EW

Bharti Airtel outperformed Sensex before the tariff hike, while it underperformed in periods after the 2021 tariff hike

![](images/9dbf3df4b5d99a22aa12efcc0b0df225ca9b8baacd3eff4179699145194857b6.jpg)

Airtel's Indian business is trading at 11.4x one-year forward EV/EBITDA (adjusted net debt)
![](images/72680f445503414498596af46d6c69cfcd10f45598bdbebc71cb5f90a555c788.jpg)

Airtel is currently trading at 9.8x EV/EBITDA (adjusted net debt), slightly above its long-term historical average
![](images/775894c5073739d0cd8f4d611e84c55942394c30a9bc38857f2e3b031ebed600.jpg)

Airtel is currently trading in the middle of the pack for FCF yield
![](images/71ebe1d213c415b851484b6273deeaed224f72299ab3991a8cd888cca36f8dba.jpg)
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research. Mean before F20 is on pre INDAS 116 EBITDA and post F20 implies reported EBITDA post adoption of INDAS 116 which bumped up the EBITDA and would have had a deflationary impact on multiples, the company reported net debt has been adjusted for the US\$1.5bn perpetual bond and provision on AGR dues for the period F17-19 Note: Pricing as of 27 Mar’24.

## Bharti Airtel: Decent FCF over F24-25e with potential to lower net debt in absolute terms

We expect US\$7.3bn of FCF generated cumulatively in the next two years

![](images/5edd0ed064da753591690d6b302a768e3b6926d01088e42ce885a3c61d5e1700.jpg)
Adjusted net debt (US\$mn) movement F23-25 - We expect significant FCF generation and the moratorium on government dues to help de-leverage the profile

## STOCK RATINGS

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell. Investors should carefully read the definitions of all ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone. In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice. An investor's decision t buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations

## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below

Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.
